### ai-appendix/definitions.md

```diff
--- r1/ai-appendix/definitions.md
+++ r2/ai-appendix/definitions.md
@@ -1,35 +1,51 @@
 # Definitions of record
 
-- **elementary causal mechanism (theta)** — `theta : A -> Delta(X)`. An element of the finite, commonly known vocabulary Theta: a map from action profiles to distributions over consequence profiles, read causally, so that it specifies the consequences that would result from every action profile, including profiles never played. marg_i theta(a) is the X_i-marginal of theta(a). Not to be confused with: An expressible theory (an assignment of mechanisms to awareness cells); the on-path conditional p_i*, which mixes mechanisms.
-- **objective assignment (tau) and objective causal system (F)** — `F(. | s, a) = tau(s)(a)`. tau: S -> Theta assigns each state its mechanism; the objective causal system is the response function F. Two states are causally equivalent if they carry the same mechanism, and P^tau is the partition of S into causal-equivalence classes. Not to be confused with: A statistical summary of on-path outcomes; an agent's awareness partition P_i (P^tau is the objective one).
-- **awareness partition (P_i)** The partition of S, P_i in Pi(S), with respect to which every consciously available object of agent i (theory, belief, conjecture, policy, question) must be measurable. An awareness cell is an implicit claim that the states it pools are causally alike. The partition is endogenous: its refinements are the formal footprint of insight. Not to be confused with: An information partition about the realized state in the usual sense; inattention or model selection, which the modeling commitments of section 3.5 distinguish from unawareness.
-- **expressible theories (M(P_i))** — `M(P_i) = {m : P_i -> Theta}`. The cell-constant mechanism assignments on P_i; theory m predicts x_i ~ marg_i m(C)(a) at cell C under profile a. Restricting theories to a single mechanism per cell is deliberate: if arbitrary per-cell distributions were expressible, no experience could reject the class. Not to be confused with: Conjectures q_i about rival play, which are strategic claims and are not tested; arbitrary conditional distributions per cell.
-- **sufficient partition** — `P sufficient for tau'  <=>  P refines P^tau'`. P is sufficient for an assignment tau' if tau' is constant on every cell of P, equivalently if P refines P^tau'. The objective assignment is expressible for agent i if and only if P_i is sufficient for tau. Not to be confused with: Statistical sufficiency; a restorative refinement, which need not be sufficient for tau.
-- **conjecture (q_i)** — `q_i : P_i -> Delta(A_-i)`. A P_i-measurable belief about rival play; a strategic claim about what other agents will do, updated empirically from observed play. Not to be confused with: A theory (a causal claim about the objective system); the diagnostic apparatus tests theories, not conjectures.
-- **deliberative record (r_i) and tokens** The retained episodes (C_{P_i}(s), a, x_i), each a token, which can be retrospectively recoded after the agent acquires a new distinction; before acquisition neither belief nor action can condition on that distinction. The analyst-level history additionally records s itself. Not to be confused with: The analyst-level history; a sufficient statistic of the data (tokens are retained individually).
-- **committed policy and subjective optimality** — `sigma_i(C) in argmax_{a_i in A_i} sum_{m in M(P_i)} mu_i(m) sum_{a_-i in A_-i} q_i(a_-i | C) E_{x ~ m(C)(a_i, a_-i)}[u_i(x_i)]`. At the start of each generation agent i commits to sigma_i: P_i -> A_i solving equation (3) at each cell, holds it for the generation's T episodes, and revises beliefs, conjectures and policies only between generations. When dissonance has made mu_i undefined and no new representation has been produced, the previously committed policy is carried forward. Not to be confused with: Objective optimality against tau; an episode-by-episode best response.
-- **generation and episode** Time is divided into generations g = 1, 2, ..., each of T episodes; in each episode a state is drawn i.i.d. from the full-support rho, actions are taken through the committed policies, and a consequence profile is realized. A generation of committed play is the stable data-generating configuration the consistency test needs. Not to be confused with: Overlapping generations of agents; the agents are the same throughout.
-- **consequence (x_i)** Everything an episode delivers to agent i (profit, sales, observed reactions), in a finite X_i; the payoff is u_i(x_i). In the running example x_i is the pair (own payoff, observed action profile). Not to be confused with: The payoff u_i(x_i) alone.
-- **on-path conditional (p_i*(. | C, a))** — `p_i*(. | C, a) = sum_{s in S(C, a)} rho(s | S(C, a)) marg_i tau(s)(a),  with S(C, a) = {s in C : a(s) = a}`. Agent i's distribution of own consequences at an observation pair (C, a) under a committed profile: a mixture of mechanisms over the states of C at which profile a is played. It is a selection object: rivals with finer partitions condition on distinctions agent i cannot see, so the within-cell state mixture varies with the profile. Not to be confused with: Any mechanism's own conditional marg_i theta(a); the paper says the two must never be conflated.
-- **consistency test, passing, and active set (Mhat_i(P_i, r_i))** — `TV(phat_i(. | C, a), marg_i m(C)(a)) <= eta + w(n_i(C, a))  for every (C, a) with n_i(C, a) >= nbar`. With tolerance eta >= 0, minimum count nbar >= 1 and a nonincreasing slack sequence w(.) -> 0, theory m passes if its prediction lies within eta + w(n_i(C, a)) in total variation of the empirical conditional at every observation pair with at least nbar observations; the active set collects the passing theories. The deterministic instance is eta = 0, nbar = 1, w = 0: exact consistency with every retained episode. Not to be confused with: Bayesian updating, which ranks expressible theories but never judges the whole class inadequate; a likelihood-ratio test against an alternative.
-- **eta-adequate and eta-inadequate theory** — `TV(p_i*(. | C, a), marg_i m(C)(a)) <= eta at every visited (C, a)`. Under a committed profile, m is eta-adequate if TV(p_i*(. | C, a), marg_i m(C)(a)) <= eta at every visited pair, and eta-inadequate if the inequality fails strictly at some visited pair. Adequacy is relative to the profile's visited pairs. Not to be confused with: Objective correctness (an adequate theory can be false at pooled states or unvisited profiles); passing the finite-sample test (adequacy is the population property the test converges to).
-- **judged updating: reference posterior (mubar_i) and acting belief (mu_i)** — `mu_i = mubar_i restricted to Mhat_i(P_i, r_i), renormalized`. Beliefs carry two tiers. The reference posterior begins at a full-support prior on M(P_i) and updates by likelihoods alone, never conditioned on the test; the acting belief is the reference posterior restricted and renormalized to the current active set, and is undefined when the active set is empty. After a refinement both tiers restart from the bridge prior on the expanded space. Not to be confused with: A single posterior conditioned on the test; ordinary Bayesian conditioning, to which judged updating reduces only in the deterministic case.
-- **causal dissonance** — `Mhat_i(P_i, r_i) = emptyset`. Agent i experiences causal dissonance at the end of a generation if its active set is empty: every expressible theory is rejected while the expressible set itself remains. The question it poses (what unrepresented difference among the pooled states explains the heterogeneity of their consequences?) has a determinate defect but not a determinate answer; it is localized, directed and semantically open. Not to be confused with: A low or declining posterior (the misspecified posterior grows confident, not alarmed); strategic error about rivals; cognitive dissonance in the psychological sense.
-- **exposed awareness and concealing profile** Under a committed profile, P_i is exposed if every m in M(P_i) is eta-inadequate. A committed profile is concealing for agent i if some expressible theory is eta-adequate under it while false as a claim about tau at some state its cells cover. Not to be confused with: Dissonance itself: exposure is a property of the profile, dissonance its almost-sure eventual consequence (Proposition 5.5).
-- **restorative and minimally restorative refinement (R(P_i, r_i))** — `P_i < P' (strict refinement) and Mhat_i(P', r_i) != emptyset`. P' is restorative at (P_i, r_i) if it strictly refines P_i and the active set on the recoded record is nonempty; it is minimally restorative if no restorative P'' lies strictly between P_i and P'. R(P_i, r_i) is the set of minimally restorative refinements. Not to be confused with: A refinement sufficient for tau (a restorative refinement may be false, as the column refinement is); the technology's range, which kappa may restrict further.
-- **coherent cell and mutually incoherent token sets** A cell of a refinement is coherent if some single mechanism passes the test on the cell's recoded observations; token sets are mutually incoherent when no single mechanism accommodates their union. Every restorative refinement must separate mutually incoherent sets. Not to be confused with: Bayesian coherence of beliefs; representation-relative coherence (the confidence a new language awards its own theories).
-- **irreparable record** — `R(P_i, r_i) = emptyset`. A dissonant record with R(P_i, r_i) = emptyset: no refinement of S restores a nonempty active set. Possible only with stochastic mechanisms (an unlucky finite sample at a single state outside the tolerance of every mechanism); then B_i = 0 and the technology fails surely. Not to be confused with: Zero-leverage dissonance, where repairs exist but none changes any represented action comparison.
-- **representation-producing technology (phi_i), truth-blind, and complexity ordering (kappa)** — `phi_i : (P_i, r_i) -> {P_i} union R(P_i, r_i)`. A possibly randomized map from (P_i, r_i) to {P_i} union R(P_i, r_i): it fails, returning the current partition, or succeeds, producing a minimally restorative refinement; it fails surely when R is empty. Truth-blind: its output distribution depends only on (P_i, r_i), never directly on tau. A complexity ordering kappa on refinements may restrict the range; it is a primitive of the technology, not an object of the agent's optimization. Not to be confused with: A prior over named future theories (the agent cannot name, weight or condition on a candidate refinement before success); a signal correlated with tau.
-- **election (epsilon_i), inquiry cost (gamma_i), and delabeled success weight (lambda_i)** — `epsilon_i = 1  iff  lambda_i B_i(r_i) >= gamma_i`. The technology operates only when agent i experiences dissonance and elects inquiry, epsilon_i = 1, at cost gamma_i > 0 in payoff units. lambda_i in (0, 1] is a subjective probability that an elected episode produces a usable refinement, defined on the event of success alone and not on the identities of possible outputs. Awareness is cumulative: acquired distinctions are never lost. Not to be confused with: A probability distribution over identified candidate refinements; the intensity or duration of inquiry, which the model does not price.
-- **pinned structure (Lambda_i(r_i)), pinned distinctions, and Theta_i(t)** — `Theta_i(t) = { m'(C'_{P'}(t)) : P' in R(P_i, r_i), m' in Mhat_i(P', r_i) }`. Each restorative refinement induces, through recoding, a partition of the retained tokens; the pinned structure is the meet (finest common coarsening) of these induced token partitions. A token rule is implementable whichever repair arrives exactly when it is Lambda_i-measurable, and the separations Lambda_i preserves are pinned distinctions. Theta_i(t) collects the mechanisms that some admissible repair's active theory assigns to token t's cell; it is nonempty because restorative active sets are nonempty. Not to be confused with: The join of the repairs (the pinned structure is their meet); the cells of any particular repair.
-- **retained-record leverage (B_i(r_i)) and the subjective recurrence premise** — `B_i(r_i) = max_{psi Lambda_i-measurable} sum_{t in r_i} min_{a_-i in A_-i} min_{theta in Theta_i(t)} ( E_{x ~ theta(psi(t), a_-i)}[u_i(x_i)] - E_{x ~ theta(sigma_i(t), a_-i)}[u_i(x_i)] )`. Under the premise that the agent values a prospective repair over one anticipated recurrence of its retained record, token for token, the leverage of inquiry at a dissonant record with R nonempty is the maximum over Lambda_i-measurable rules psi of the summed worst-case gain of psi(t) over the carried-forward action sigma_i(t), the worst case taken over rival actions and over Theta_i(t). B_i = 0 when there is no dissonance or when R is empty. It is computable from the record with (Theta, u_i) and invariant across repairs. Not to be confused with: The expected value of information; the objective value of repair (B_i understates it when consequences conceal forgone values); a per-episode or per-generation rate (the horizon is one recurrence of the whole record).
-- **participation rule and represented value of inquiry** — `lambda_i B_i(r_i) - gamma_i`. Electing inquiry has represented value lambda_i B_i(r_i) - gamma_i, and agent i elects if and only if lambda_i B_i(r_i) >= gamma_i. It is a non-Bayesian criterion operating where the posterior is undefined, quantifying only over retained tokens and their recorded outcomes. Not to be confused with: A Bayesian expected-utility comparison over candidate theories.
-- **counterfactual transparency** The degree to which consequences identify forgone values; it lives in the inner minimum over Theta_i(t) of equation (6). When a lost episode's record pins the mechanism every repair must assign it (winner-take-all markets), Theta_i(t) is a singleton and the bound is exact; when consequences conceal forgone values, Theta_i(t) is large and B_i understates the objective value of repair. Not to be confused with: Exposure (whether play makes the falsity of the language visible), a separate dimension of an environment's diagnosticity.
-- **formulation, bridge prior (mu_i^+), and representation-relative coherence** After successful production yields P_i', formulation selects a theory m' in Mhat_i(P_i', r_i) on the recoded record, and the agent forms a full-support bridge prior mu_i^+ in Delta(M(P_i')), not determined by the pre-dissonance belief, from which ordinary updating resumes. Representation-relative coherence is mu_i^+ renormalized on the active set: the confidence the new language awards its own theories. Not to be confused with: Warrant (what the evidence licenses); a Bayesian update of the old belief (the domains differ).
-- **reflective assessment problem ((T, nu)), selection-adjusted warrant (W), and warrant gap (G)** — `G = Coh(m') - W`. A reflective assessment problem is a finite set T of candidate objective assignments with a full-support reflective prior nu. Given the record and a successful production-and-formulation event, W is the nu-posterior probability that m' is objectively correct, i.e. tau'(s) = m'(C_{P_i'}(s)) for all s, conditional on the record and on the production event. G is the representation-relative coherence probability of m' minus W. Not to be confused with: The agent's own posterior (the assessment is generally not expressible by the agent, Corollary 8.3); post-insight confidence.
-- **self-confirming unawareness equilibrium (SCUE)** A profile of epistemic states xi_i = (P_i, r_i, mubar_i, q_i, sigma_i), with acting beliefs by judged updating, such that under the play it induces (i) each sigma_i is subjectively optimal (equation (3)); (ii) each q_i agrees with the play induced by sigma_-i at every on-path cell; (iii) no dissonance occurs: each active set is almost surely eventually nonempty and each acting belief is supported on eta-adequate theories; (iv) no agent elects inquiry: lambda_i B_i(r_i) < gamma_i along the induced records. The record belongs in the state. Not to be confused with: Nash equilibrium; self-confirming equilibrium of the fixed-language tradition (conditions (i)-(ii) only); a statement that play converges.
-- **Bayesian world and dissonant stasis** An agent lives in a Bayesian world when it is eventually never dissonant: models fit, questions are dead, inquiry is priced below cost. It is in dissonant stasis when dissonance recurs while lambda_i B_i < gamma_i, in particular whenever B_i = 0, as at irreparable records. Not to be confused with: Convergence of play; a pathology of the model (zero-leverage stasis is rational anomaly tolerance).
-- **diagnostic environment and payoff-relevantly false theory** A theory is payoff-relevantly false if it is false at some state where the truth would change its holder's optimal action. The primitives (Theta, tau, rho, u) are diagnostic if, under every profile sustainable in a SCUE, every payoff-relevantly false expressible theory is eta-inadequate. Not to be confused with: Exposure under one particular profile; an environment in which no false theory can be held at all.
-- **recurrent and frozen observation pair (appendix A)** Under an eventually constant committed profile sigma*, a pair (C, a) is recurrent if S_{sigma*}(C, a) != emptyset and frozen otherwise. A frozen pair is visited at most finitely often, so its test constraint is eventually constant; a recurrent pair's count diverges almost surely. Not to be confused with: Recurrent states of a Markov chain.
-- **row law, column world, factor refinements, and beta (running example)** — `tau(s_rc) = theta^r;  tau'(s_rc) = theta^c`. In the four-state laboratory the objective assignment is the row law tau(s_rc) = theta^r, and the column world is the observationally equivalent assignment tau'(s_rc) = theta^c. The row and column partitions are the one-coordinate (factor) refinements a factor-seeking technology produces. The demand parameter beta in [0, 1] is the share of unit value a customer pays when the firms pool on the wrong architecture; the baseline sets beta = 1. Not to be confused with: The row and column partitions as awareness (either firm may hold either); the true law with the agent's theory.
-- **insight (formal footprint)** Everything the paper calls insight is the production of a distinction that separates states carrying different mechanisms: the refinement of the awareness partition the technology returns. The conscious act itself is not represented; its products and its price are. Not to be confused with: Evidence (Theorem 8.2: the insight is not evidence); expansion of the mechanism vocabulary Theta, which the paper leaves outside.
+- **elementary causal mechanism** — `theta: A -> Delta(X)`. An element of the finite vocabulary Theta, known to all agents from the outset: a map from action profiles to distributions over consequences, the reduced form of whatever detailed causal model underlies the state-action-consequence relation. Not to be confused with: A theory (an assignment of mechanisms to awareness cells); the paper imposes no structural-causal-model formalism, and expansion of the mechanism vocabulary itself is outside the paper.
+- **objective assignment (objective causal system)** — `tau: S -> Theta; x ~ tau(s)(a)`. The primitive tau assigning a mechanism to each state; tau(s)(a) is the distribution over consequences determined by the state and action profile of a period, defined also at unplayed profiles and unvisited states. Never accessed by agents. Not to be confused with: On-path conditionals, which mix mechanisms according to which states co-occur with which play.
+- **causal equivalence; causal-equivalence partition** — `s ~ s' iff tau(s) = tau(s')`. Two states are causally equivalent if the objective assignment gives them the same mechanism; P^tau is the partition of S into causal-equivalence classes. Not to be confused with: Observational equivalence of two worlds (agreement of consequence laws at reachable state-profile pairs).
+- **awareness partition** — `P_i in Pi(S)`. Agent i's partition P_i of S. Theories and policies are defined on its cells, conjectures condition only on its cells, and beliefs range only over theories expressible on it. An awareness cell is an implicit causal claim that the states it pools are alike in how actions produce consequences. Not to be confused with: An information partition about which state occurred, or inattention: the agent perceives its current cell, but awareness bounds which distinctions it can represent at all.
+- **expressible theories** — `M(P_i) = {m : P_i -> Theta}`. The cell-constant mechanism assignments on the awareness partition; theory m predicts x_i ~ marg_i m(C)(a) at cell C under profile a. Restricting theories to a single mechanism per cell is substantive and deliberate. Not to be confused with: Arbitrary conditional distributions per cell (a saturated model space that no experience could reject); conjectures about rival play.
+- **sufficient partition** — `P refines P^tau'`. Partition P is sufficient for an assignment tau' if tau' is constant on every cell of P, equivalently if P refines P^tau'. The objective assignment is expressible for agent i iff P_i is sufficient for tau. Not to be confused with: Statistical sufficiency.
+- **conjecture** — `q_i : P_i -> Delta(A_-i)`. Agent i's strategic claim about rival play, conditioning on its own awareness cells and updated empirically from observed play. Not to be confused with: A theory, which is a causal claim about the objective system; the diagnostic apparatus tests theories, not conjectures.
+- **deliberative record** — `r_i = multiset of (C_{P_i}(s), a, x_i)`. The multiset r_i of retained tokens (C_{P_i}(s), a, x_i); the only record on which the agent's beliefs, tests and decisions may depend. Not to be confused with: The analyst record r_bar_i, which additionally carries the underlying state of each token.
+- **analyst record** — `r_bar_i = multiset of (s, C_{P_i}(s), a, x_i)`. The record r_bar_i that additionally carries the underlying state s of each token. Used only by analyst-level constructions (restorative correspondence, irreparability, warrant accounting) and consumed by the representation-producing technology; never by deliberation. Not to be confused with: Anything the agent can deliberate on.
+- **recodability** On acquiring a refinement P' the agent can classify every retained token under P', producing a recoded deliberative record r_i^{P'}; recoding is total and infallible once the predicate is held and unavailable before. Not to be confused with: Pre-insight access to the distinguishing predicate.
+- **standing policy; belief-optimal policy** — `sigma_i_star(C) in argmax_{a_i} sum_m mu_i(m) sum_{a_-i} q_i(a_-i|C) E_{x~m(C)(a_i,a_-i)}[pi_i(x_i)]`. The standing policy sigma_i is carried from one period to the next. The belief-optimal policy plays, at each cell, an action maximizing subjective expected payoff under the acting belief and conjecture. It is undefined when the acting belief is undefined, and then the standing policy is carried forward. Not to be confused with: The policy actually played, which changes only when the switching hurdle is cleared.
+- **subjective per-period value** — `U_i(sigma|mu_i,q_i) = sum_C rho(C) sum_m mu_i(m) sum_{a_-i} q_i(a_-i|C) E_{x~m(C)(sigma(C),a_-i)}[pi_i(x_i)], rho(C) = sum_{s in C} rho(s)`. Agent i's subjective per-period value of a policy given its acting belief and conjecture, weighting cells by their known probabilities. Not to be confused with: Objective expected payoff.
+- **valuation horizon; switching hurdle; policy inertia** — `switch iff T*(U_i(sigma_i_star|mu_i,q_i) - U_i(sigma_i|mu_i,q_i)) > kappa_i`. A lumpy one-time cost is valued against the gain it produces over a common undiscounted horizon of T periods. The agent replaces sigma_i by sigma_i_star iff the horizon-T gain exceeds the one-time switching hurdle kappa_i >= 0; the inaction band is the source of policy inertia, which is an outcome rather than an assumption. Not to be confused with: Discounting (T is a bounded-rationality stand-in for it).
+- **on-path conditional** — `p_i*(.|C,a) = sum_{s in S(C,a)} rho(s|S(C,a)) * marg_i tau(s)(a), S(C,a) = {s in C : a(s) = a}`. Over a stable stretch with constant standing profile, agent i's distribution of own consequences at an observation pair (C,a): a selection object mixing the mechanisms of the states in C that receive profile a. Not to be confused with: Any mechanism's own conditional.
+- **exact calibration** — `eta = 0, w(n) = 0 for all n`. Deterministic mechanisms with adequacy tolerance eta = 0 and finite-sample slack w = 0, in which tests reject at the first impossible observation and false alarms are zero; the running example uses it. Not to be confused with: The general stochastic case, in which every definition is stated.
+- **adequate; eta-adequate; eta-inadequate** — `TV(p_i*(.|C,a), marg_i m(C)(a)) <= eta`. Theory m is adequate at a visited pair (C,a) when the total-variation distance between the on-path conditional and m's prediction is at most eta. Under a standing profile, m is eta-adequate if it is adequate at every visited pair and eta-inadequate if it is inadequate at some visited pair. Not to be confused with: Objective truth (an adequate theory can be false at pooled states or unvisited profiles); retrospective fit to a fixed record.
+- **fit (retrospective)** — `TV(p_hat_i(.|C,a), marg_i m(C)(a)) <= eta + w(n_i(C,a))`. Scoring a candidate against a fixed record rather than a stream, using counts n_i(C,a), empirical conditionals p_hat_i, and a nonnegative, nonincreasing slack w(.) -> 0. Not to be confused with: Sequential adequacy testing.
+- **evidence process; dissonance capacity** — `reject m when log(E_i^m) >= b_i; Pr(sup_t E_i^m(t) >= exp(b_i)) <= exp(-b_i)`. For each admitted theory, a nonnegative process E_i^m initialized at 1. It is a supermartingale under adequacy and diverges almost surely under recurrent strict inadequacy (Appendix A betting mixture; a zero-likelihood detector in the deterministic case). The theory is rejected permanently at the first period with log E_i^m >= b_i, and the capacity b_i > 0 is the agent's evidence threshold for abandoning a theory. Not to be confused with: The Bayesian posterior: the reference posterior is never conditioned on the test.
+- **active set** — `M_hat_i(P_i, r_i)`. The theories admitted when the current language was initialized that have not subsequently been rejected by the sequential test. It only shrinks, and rejected theories never re-enter. Not to be confused with: The expressible set M(P_i); the support of the posterior.
+- **judged updating (reference posterior and acting belief)** — `mu_i(m) = mu_bar_i(m) / sum_{m~ in M_hat_i} mu_bar_i(m~) for m in M_hat_i`. Beliefs have two tiers. The reference posterior mu_bar_i starts at a full-support prior and updates by token likelihoods, never conditioned on the test. The acting belief mu_i is the reference posterior restricted and renormalized to the current active set, and it is undefined when the active set is empty. Not to be confused with: Ordinary Bayesian conditioning, with which it coincides only in the deterministic case while the normalizer is positive.
+- **causal dissonance** — `M_hat_i(P_i, r_i) = {}`. Agent i experiences causal dissonance at any period at which its active set is empty: no theory admitted on the current language remains unrejected. Dissonance poses the question whether an unrepresented distinction among the pooled states can reconcile the record, and if so what it is. Not to be confused with: A low posterior or surprise (posterior mass always sums to one); proof of objective representational insufficiency, which holds only under exact deterministic testing.
+- **exposed; concealing** Under a standing profile, awareness P_i is exposed if every expressible theory is eta-inadequate. A standing profile is concealing for agent i if some expressible theory is eta-adequate under it while false as a claim about tau at some state its cells cover. Not to be confused with: Dissonance itself: exposure yields dissonance almost surely only under the activity proviso of Proposition 6.5.
+- **repair test; repair-consistent active set** — `M_hat_i^R(P', r_bar_i) = {m' in M(P') : TV(p_hat_i(.|C',a), marg_i m'(C')(a)) <= eta + w(n_i(C',a)) for every (C',a) with n_i(C',a) > 0}`. For a candidate refinement P', the theories on P' that fit the recoded analyst record at every nonempty recoded cell-profile pair. Refinement can reconcile observations but cannot delete their constraints. Not to be confused with: The deliberative grouping test, which applies only where counts are at least n_bar.
+- **restorative; minimally restorative refinement** — `restorative iff M_hat_i^R(P', r_bar_i) != {}`. A strict refinement P' of P_i is restorative if its repair-consistent active set is nonempty, and minimally restorative if no restorative partition lies strictly between P_i and P'. R(P_i, r_bar_i) is the set of minimally restorative refinements. This is an analyst-level object. Not to be confused with: An objectively correct refinement.
+- **irreparable record** — `R(P_i, r_bar_i) = {}`. A dissonant record with no minimally restorative refinement. It is possible only with stochastic mechanisms, it is analyst-level, and the agent cannot in general tell it from a repairable record. Not to be confused with: A record whose repair is merely unpriced or abandoned.
+- **representation-producing technology; truth-blind** — `phi_i : (P_i, r_bar_i) -> {P_i} union R(P_i, r_bar_i)`. A possibly randomized map from the analyst record to the current partition (failure) or to a minimally restorative refinement (success). It is truth-blind: its output distribution depends only on (P_i, r_bar_i), never directly on tau. A complexity ordering on refinements may restrict its range. Not to be confused with: A model of the semantic creative act (treated as a primitive); a pre-insight menu the agent can name, price, or condition on.
+- **formulation rule (record-measurable and truth-blind)** — `psi_i(. | P_i', r_i^{P_i'}) in Delta(M_hat_i^R(P_i', r_bar_i))`. A possibly randomized kernel selecting a theory from the repair-consistent active set on the refined language. It is record-measurable and truth-blind if it depends only on its displayed arguments and on internal randomness independent of the objective assignment and all draws; a truth-blind inquiry-and-formulation rule is such a pair (phi_i, psi_i). Not to be confused with: Transmission from an informed source, which can be correlated with the objective assignment.
+- **bridge prior** — `mu_i^+ in Delta(M(P_i'))`. A fresh full-support prior on the expanded language, formed after successful production, from which ordinary updating resumes; Bayesian coherence does not determine it from the pre-dissonance belief. Not to be confused with: A Bayesian update of the old prior (the domains differ).
+- **costly, elective inquiry** — `cost gamma_i > 0 per election`. The technology operates only when agent i is dissonant and elects inquiry, at cost gamma_i > 0 in payoff units. On failure or abstention the standing policy is carried forward, and acquired distinctions are never lost. Not to be confused with: Continuous search or experimentation within a fixed language.
+- **delabeled achievability weight** — `lambda_i^(k) in (0,1], nonincreasing in k, lambda_i^(k) -> 0`. A subjective probability that an election produces a refinement delivering the estimated leverage. It is defined on that event alone, not on the identities of possible outputs, and is nonincreasing in the count k of consecutive failed elections, tending to 0. It is awareness of unawareness in delabeled form. Not to be confused with: A prior over named candidate refinements; a mere success probability.
+- **deliberative grouping; coherent group** — `Theta_i(g) = {theta in Theta : TV(p_hat_g(.|a), marg_i theta(a)) <= eta + w(n_g(a)) for every a with n_g(a) >= n_bar}`. A deliberative grouping G is a partition of the record's tokens. Theta_i(g) is the set of mechanisms that pass the grouping test on group g wherever the count is at least n_bar. A group is coherent if Theta_i(g) is nonempty, and a grouping is coherent if all its groups are; G_i(r_i) is the finite family of coherent groupings. It uses no state labels. Not to be confused with: A partition of S or a candidate refinement: a coherent grouping is the agent's hypothesis about which particulars share a mechanism and need not be realizable.
+- **per-token robust gain** — `Delta_i^G(t,a_i) = min_{a_-i} min_{theta in Theta_i(g(t))} (E_{x~theta(a_i,a_-i)}[pi_i(x_i)] - E_{x~theta(sigma_i(t),a_-i)}[pi_i(x_i)])`. The worst case, over rival responses and over the mechanisms the group's record leaves open, of the payoff gain from proposing action a_i rather than the standing action at the token's cell. Not to be confused with: An expected gain under a belief.
+- **robust fine-contingent value; current-language benchmark; gross representational increment** — `Lfine = sum_C sum_g max_{a_i} sum_{t in r_i(C,g)} Delta_i^G(t,a_i); Lcur = sum_C max_{a_i} sum_g sum_{t in r_i(C,g)} Delta_i^G(t,a_i); J = Lfine - Lcur`. L_fine lets action vary by each nonempty current-cell/group intersection. L_cur requires one action per current awareness cell, evaluated under the same coherent grouping. J is their difference. Not to be confused with: A new dissonant policy-switching rule: L_cur is a counterfactual valuation benchmark.
+- **deliberative leverage (value of a question)** — `B = (T/r)*max({0} union {J(G) : G in G_i(r_i)}) if M_hat_i(P_i,r_i) = {}, else 0; for the best grouping B = (T/r)*(Lfine - Lcur)`. At a dissonant record, T over the record size times the largest gross representational increment over coherent groupings, with outside option 0; zero when the active set is nonempty. It prices the incremental gross action value of making a coherent grouping actionable, on the premise that the empirical token distribution recurs. Not to be confused with: Value of information or uncertainty reduction (it prices action separation); a net expected payoff after switching costs; an expected value over named candidate answers.
+- **pointwise transparency** — `|Theta_i({t})| = 1 for every retained token t`. In the deterministic zero-tolerance case with n_bar = 1 and w = 0, a record is pointwise transparent if every retained token pins a single mechanism. The leverage estimate is then exact and repair-invariant. Not to be confused with: Determinism alone, which certifies separability of conflicting tokens but does not make every grouping realizable.
+- **representation-relative coherence** — `Coh_i(O_i) = mu_i^+(m') / sum_{m~ in M_hat_i^R(P_i', r_bar_i)} mu_i^+(m~)`. The probability that the initial acting belief on the refined language (the bridge prior restricted and renormalized to the repair-consistent active set) assigns to the formulated theory. Not to be confused with: Selection-adjusted warrant.
+- **reflective assessment problem** An analyst-level pair (calT, nu): a finite set of candidate objective assignments and a full-support reflective prior. Conditioning uses the analyst record and the complete pre-production inquiry history h_i. Not to be confused with: The agent's own bridge prior or acting belief.
+- **selection-adjusted warrant** — `W(o) = nu(A_o | r_bar_i, h_i, O_i = o), A_o = {tau' in calT : tau'(s) = m_o(C_{P'}(s)) for every s}`. The reflective probability, given the analyst record, the inquiry history and the realized output, that the formulated theory is objectively correct, relative to the specified assessment problem. Not to be confused with: Representation-relative coherence (the agent's confidence); retrospective fit.
+- **signed warrant gap** — `gap = Coh - W`. Coherence minus warrant at a realized output; its sign is unrestricted in general. Not to be confused with: An error of the agent's updating.
+- **self-confirming unawareness equilibrium (SCUE)** — `(i) T*(U_i(sigma_i_star|mu_i,q_i) - U_i(sigma_i|mu_i,q_i)) <= kappa_i; (iv) lambda_i^(k_i)*B_hat_i(r_i) < gamma_i`. A profile of epistemic states (P_i, r_i, mu_bar_i, q_i, sigma_i, k_i) such that, under the play it induces: (i) no switch clears the hurdle; (ii) conjectures agree in the limit with induced play at on-path cells; (iii) each active set is almost surely eventually nonempty and acting-belief mass on eta-inadequate theories vanishes; (iv) no agent elects inquiry. Not to be confused with: A self-confirming equilibrium of the fixed-language tradition (SCUE adds language survival and no priced question); convergence of play.
+- **partial SCUE** A configuration in which the SCUE conditions hold for every non-stasis agent, with the stasis agents' constant carried-forward policies taken as fixed environment. Not to be confused with: A full SCUE.
+- **dissonant stasis; absorbing profile** — `lambda_i^(k_i)*B_hat_i(r_i) < gamma_i`. An agent is in dissonant stasis from some period on if dissonance recurs while it never both elects and succeeds: either its question is unpriced (zero leverage, an abandoned question, possibly at an irreparable record) or elected production always fails. It carries its standing policy with a standing anomaly. A population profile is absorbing if every agent is eventually either in a SCUE role (never dissonant) or in dissonant stasis. Not to be confused with: SCUE, in which a represented model class survives.
+- **enlightened inertia** — `T*(U_i(sigma_i_star) - U_i(sigma_i)) <= kappa_i after refinement`. Having acquired a refinement and an improved model, the agent carries its old policy forward because the realized horizon-T gain of the recommended switch does not clear the switching hurdle. Not to be confused with: Ignorance or dissonant stasis.
+- **abandoned question; rational anomaly tolerance** — `lambda_i^(k)*B_hat_i < gamma_i`. An abandoned question is a repairable, positively valued defect that the agent stops pursuing because repeated failure has driven lambda_i^(k)*B_hat_i below cost. Anomaly tolerance is rational when the agent's coherent groupings carry no gross representational leverage. Not to be confused with: Indifference or a pathology of the model.
+- **payoff-relevant policy error** — `E_{x~tau(s)(a^sigma(s))}[pi_i(x_i)] < max_{a_i~} E_{x~tau(s)(a_i~, a_-i^sigma(s))}[pi_i(x_i)]`. At state s the standing action is strictly suboptimal under objective evaluation, given the rivals' induced actions. Not to be confused with: A change in the set of optimal actions that leaves the standing action optimal.
+- **diagnostic at tolerance eta; strictly punishing** — `min_{m in M(P_i)} TV(p_i*(.|C_{P_i}(s), a^sigma(s)), marg_i m(C_{P_i}(s))(a^sigma(s))) > eta`. Primitives are diagnostic if, for every profile satisfying SCUE conditions (i)-(ii), any payoff-relevant policy error implies the erring agent's awareness is exposed. They are strictly punishing if at every payoff-relevant policy error the on-path conditional is more than eta from every expressible prediction. Not to be confused with: Global openness of diagnosticity (only local robustness of strict margins is claimed).
+- **insight** — `P_i -> P_i' with P_i' strictly refining P_i`. Represented formally as the production of a distinction, a refinement of the awareness partition; the model explains when insight is demanded, what constrains it, what it is worth and what its output warrants. With the mechanism vocabulary fixed, insight means discovering which differences matter. Not to be confused with: The conscious creative or semantic act (a primitive); expansion of the mechanism vocabulary.
+- **novelty (agent-relative)** Refinement makes an analyst-represented distinction available to the agent but does not create objective states or elementary mechanisms. Not to be confused with: Creation of the analyst's ontology or a new kind of causal mechanism.
+- **row law; column world; demand parameter beta (running example)** — `tau(s_rc) = theta^r; tau'(s_rc) = theta^c; pooled payoff on the wrong architecture = beta/2`. In Example 1, the objective assignment is the row law (the row coordinate is causally relevant); the column world is the alternative assignment. Under theta^a, differentiated play gives payoff 1 to the firm offering a and 0 to its rival; pooling on a gives 1/2 each; pooling on the other architecture gives beta/2 each. Under theta^n each receives 1/2. Not to be confused with: General results; the numbers are example-specific.
```

```diff
--- r1/ai-appendix/procedure.md
+++ r2/ai-appendix/procedure.md
@@ -1,18 +1,21 @@
 # Procedure
 
-1. Fix the primitives: agents N = {1, ..., n}; a finite state space S with full-support rho; finite action sets A_i, A = prod A_i; finite consequence spaces X_i with payoffs u_i: X_i -> R; a finite mechanism vocabulary Theta of maps theta: A -> Delta(X); and the objective assignment tau: S -> Theta, giving F(. | s, a) = tau(s)(a). Time runs in generations of T episodes; states are drawn i.i.d. each episode.
-2. For each agent fix the awareness partition P_i and form the expressible theories M(P_i) = {m : P_i -> Theta}; fix a full-support reference prior mubar_i on M(P_i) and a P_i-measurable conjecture q_i: P_i -> Delta(A_-i).
-3. At the start of each generation compute the acting belief mu_i (mubar_i restricted and renormalized to the current active set) and choose the committed policy sigma_i cell by cell from equation (3): sigma_i(C) maximizes sum_m mu_i(m) sum_{a_-i} q_i(a_-i | C) E_{x ~ m(C)(a_i, a_-i)}[u_i(x_i)]. If the active set is empty and no new representation has been produced, carry the previous policy forward. Hold the policy for T episodes.
-4. From the committed profile sigma derive the deterministic profile a(s) = (sigma_j(C_{P_j}(s)))_j at each state, the visited sets S(C, a) = {s in C : a(s) = a}, and the on-path conditional p_i*(. | C, a) = sum_{s in S(C, a)} rho(s | S(C, a)) marg_i tau(s)(a) (equation (4)).
-5. At the end of each generation record the tokens (C_{P_i}(s), a, x_i), compute counts n_i(C, a) and empirical conditionals phat_i(. | C, a), and apply the test of equation (5) to each m in M(P_i): m passes if TV(phat_i(. | C, a), marg_i m(C)(a)) <= eta + w(n_i(C, a)) at every pair with n_i(C, a) >= nbar. The active set is the set of passing theories. Update the reference posterior by likelihoods alone. In the deterministic instance (eta = 0, nbar = 1, w = 0) passing is exact consistency with every retained token.
-6. Declare causal dissonance when the active set is empty. To decide whether dissonance will occur under a fixed profile, check exposure: P_i is exposed if every m in M(P_i) is eta-inadequate, i.e. TV(p_i*(. | C, a), marg_i m(C)(a)) > eta at some visited pair; under the genericity hypothesis (no distance equals eta exactly) Lemma 5.2 gives almost-sure eventual acceptance of adequate and rejection of inadequate theories, and Proposition 5.5 gives dissonance from some generation onward almost surely iff P_i is exposed.
-7. At a dissonant record enumerate the strict refinements P' of P_i. Recode the tokens to P' using the analyst-level states. A cell is coherent if some single mechanism passes the test on its recoded tokens; by Proposition 6.2 the recoded active set is nonempty iff every cell is coherent, so P' is restorative iff every cell is coherent. R(P_i, r_i) is the set of restorative refinements with no restorative refinement strictly between P_i and them. If R is empty the record is irreparable: B_i = 0 and the technology fails surely.
-8. For nonidentification compare two assignments tau and tau' that induce the same record distribution under the played profiles: any truth-blind rule has the same output distribution in both worlds, and if their eta-adequate sets at the visited pairs are disjoint no truth-blind rule is adequate with probability one in both (Theorem 6.5). A partition sufficient for every assignment in a candidate set T must refine the join over T of the causal-equivalence partitions P^tau' (Proposition 6.6).
-9. Compute leverage. For each token t let Theta_i(t) be the set of mechanisms that some repair P' in R, through a theory in its recoded active set, assigns to t's cell. Form the token partition each P' induces and take their meet Lambda_i(r_i). Maximize equation (6) over Lambda_i-measurable rules psi: B_i(r_i) = max_psi sum_t min_{a_-i} min_{theta in Theta_i(t)} ( E_{x ~ theta(psi(t), a_-i)}[u_i(x_i)] - E_{x ~ theta(sigma_i(t), a_-i)}[u_i(x_i)] ). psi = sigma_i is feasible, so B_i >= 0. The agent elects inquiry iff lambda_i B_i(r_i) >= gamma_i (Proposition 7.4).
-10. On an elected and successful production the technology returns some P' in R (possibly restricted by kappa). Formulation selects m' in Mhat_i(P', r_i) on the recoded record; a full-support bridge prior mu_i^+ on M(P') is formed; both belief tiers restart from it; representation-relative coherence is mu_i^+ renormalized on the active set, evaluated at m'.
-11. Compute warrant. Choose a reflective assessment (T, nu). The posterior on tau' in T is proportional to nu(tau') times the record's likelihood under tau' times the probability of the production event given the record; under truth blindness the last factor is constant across T and cancels, and post-restoration fit has likelihood one, so W equals the ordinary reflective posterior on the record (Theorem 8.2); among candidates with equal record likelihood W equals the prior. The warrant gap is G = Coh(m') - W. The agent can express the assessment only if P_i refines the join of the candidates' partitions (Corollary 8.3).
-12. For the dynamics (Theorem 9.4) bound the total cell count sum_i |P_i|, an integer in [n, n|S|] that strictly increases at every successful production: at most n(|S| - 1) refinements occur. Enumerate elections with success indicators Z_k; each election occurs at a dissonant record with R nonempty, so its conditional success probability is at least pi_lower, and by Levy's extension of the Borel-Cantelli lemma infinitely many elections would give infinitely many successes, which the cell-count bound forbids: finitely many elections. After G*, the larger of the last refinement and last election generation, no language changes and no cost is paid. On the event of an eventually constant profile sigma*, frozen pairs' constraints are eventually constant, recurrent pairs' empirical conditionals converge to p_i* under sigma*, generic eta makes each constraint eventually permanently satisfied or failed, so each active set is eventually constant and eta-adequate at recurrent pairs; a nonempty terminal active set means never dissonant, an empty one means dissonant stasis with lambda_i B_i < gamma_i.
-13. To verify a SCUE (Definition 9.1) check, under the induced play: (i) each sigma_i solves equation (3); (ii) each q_i agrees with induced play at every on-path cell; (iii) each active set is almost surely eventually nonempty and each acting belief is supported on eta-adequate theories; (iv) lambda_i B_i(r_i) < gamma_i along the induced records. Proposition 9.5(i): an almost surely converging configuration limits, off the agents in dissonant stasis, to a SCUE. Proposition 9.5(ii): a SCUE whose play generates only B_i = 0 records, with strict optimality under every conjecture and active theory, persists surely under deterministic mechanisms; under stochastic mechanisms, with every active theory eta'-adequate for some eta' < eta and strict optimality extended to (eta + epsilon)-adequate theories for some epsilon in (0, 2(eta - eta')], choose N(delta) so that w(n) <= epsilon/2 for n >= N and sum_{n >= N} K exp(-n epsilon^2/2) <= delta/P (K the finite-alphabet Hoeffding constant, P the number of recurrent pairs); then with probability at least 1 - delta play never changes and the SCUE conditions hold thereafter.
-14. For where error lives (Propositions 9.8 and 9.11) use condition (iii): surviving theories are eta-adequate at visited pairs, so falsity hides only at pooled states whose mixture imitates a single mechanism at the visited profiles and at unvisited profiles. If the environment is diagnostic (every payoff-relevantly false theory is eta-inadequate under every SCUE-sustainable profile) no SCUE carries payoff-relevant error; otherwise such error requires a concealing profile to be sustained.
-15. Running example, all exact (eta = 0, nbar = 1, w = 0): S = {s00, s01, s10, s11} with uniform rho; A_i = {0, 1}; Theta = {theta^0, theta^1, theta^n}; under theta^a, differentiated firms give 1 to the firm offering a and 0 to its rival, pooling on a gives 1/2 each, pooling on the other architecture gives beta/2 each; under theta^n each firm gets 1/2; x_i = (own payoff, observed action profile); tau(s_rc) = theta^r; both firms start at P_i^0 = {S}. Steps: (a) dominance: with p^a the belief mass on theories assigning theta^a at the cell, the gain of action 0 over 1 is p^0/2 - (1 - beta/2) p^1 against a rival playing 0 and (1 - beta/2) p^0 - p^1/2 against a rival playing 1; action 0 is strictly dominant iff p^0 > (2 - beta) p^1, at beta = 1 simple belief asymmetry. (b) discovery: firm 1 on 0, firm 2 on 1, s00 and s11 served; firm 1 gets 1 at s00 and 0 at s11 (firm 2 the reverse); m^0, m^1, m^n each fail one episode, so both active sets empty. Pooled play would give 1/2 at every episode and pass all three theories (concealing). (c) repairs: 15 partitions of four states; those keeping s00 and s11 together are the 5 partitions of three elements, so 10 separate them; the minimal ones are the row partition, the column partition and the two isolating one labeled state (4), and 15 - 4 = 11 are excluded. The column world tau'(s_rc) = theta^c reproduces the record; a rule formulating the row refinement with probability q succeeds with q in the row world and 1 - q in the column world, worst case min(q, 1 - q) <= 1/2; the join of the row and column partitions is the four singletons. (d) leverage: each firm's committed action disagrees with one revealed label; implementing it at the recurrence gains 1/2 - 0 if the rival plays the label and 1 - 1/2 if the rival plays the committed action, so B_i = 1/2 and the firm elects iff lambda_i/2 >= gamma_i. Validation (firm 1 row-refined on the row law, firm 2 column-refined on the column law, all four states served on top of the retained record): B_1 = 0, B_2 = 2 x 1/2 = 1. (e) warrant: the column (or row) refinement pins a unique theory, coherence 1; with T = {row world, column world} and nu = 1/2, equal record likelihoods give W = 1/2 and G = 1/2; a differentiated off-diagonal observation makes the reflective posterior degenerate. (f) trap: both column-refined and pooled on the column policy; at beta = 1 every payoff is 1/2, equal to the column theory's prediction, active sets never empty, B_i = 0: a SCUE with payoff-relevant error. For beta < 1 an off-diagonal state pays beta/2 against a prediction of 1/2 (shortfall (1 - beta)/2, destroyed value 1 - beta): dissonance, positive leverage and a degenerate reflective posterior. (g) persistence: firm 1 on the row law, firm 2 constant: per-episode payoffs (1/2 + 1/2 + 1 + 1)/4 = 3/4 and (1/2 + 1/2 + 0 + 0)/4 = 1/4; firm 2's record rejects all three constant theories; stasis whenever gamma_2 > lambda_2 B_2.
-16. Reproduce the numbers with materials/lab.py: its anchor-facing functions (dominance_gain, dominance_threshold, discovery_payoff, discovery_active_theories, count_partitions, minimal_repairs, excluded_partitions, worst_case_success, robust_partition_cells, extrapolation_errors, unit_gain, discovery_leverage, validation_leverage, election_value, coherence, warrant, warrant_gap, trap_payoff, trap_prediction, trap_prediction_error, destroyed_value, active_theories, trap_leverage, persistence_payoff, persistence_leverage, refinement_bound) take keyword parameters named by the paper's symbols, default to the paper's values, use exact arithmetic and return floats; running the module prints each value beside the number the text states.
+1. Fix the primitives (held constant throughout): agents N with n >= 2; a finite state set S drawn i.i.d. from full-support rho; finite actions A_i and consequences X; payoffs pi_i; the mechanism vocabulary Theta with theta: A -> Delta(X); and the objective assignment tau: S -> Theta. Each agent holds an awareness partition P_i and expressible theories M(P_i) = {m: P_i -> Theta}. It observes its cell at the start of each period and (a, x_i) at the end, and retains the deliberative record r_i. The analyst record r_bar_i adds state labels.
+2. Choice within a fixed language: given the acting belief mu_i and the empirical conjecture q_i, compute sigma_i_star cellwise by equation (2) and the subjective value U_i by equation (3). Replace sigma_i by sigma_i_star iff T*(U_i(sigma_i_star) - U_i(sigma_i)) > kappa_i; otherwise, or when mu_i is undefined, carry sigma_i forward.
+3. Proposition 5.1: Bayesian or judged conditioning reweights a distribution on its fixed domain M(P_i). Every element is cell-constant, so every posterior and every policy optimal against one is P_i-measurable, and no update enlarges the domain.
+4. On a stable stretch with constant profile sigma, each state receives a(s) = (sigma_j(C_{P_j}(s)))_j. Compute agent i's on-path conditional p_i*(.|C,a) by equation (4), which mixes the mechanisms of the states in C receiving profile a.
+5. Testing (Definition 6.1, Appendix A, Lemma D.1): for each admitted theory, run the evidence process E_i^m, a convex mixture of betting factors F = 1 + lambda*(1{x in D} - (q(D) + eta)) at each visited pair, initialized at 1. It is a supermartingale under adequacy, so Ville gives Pr(sup E >= exp(b_i)) <= E(t_0)*exp(-b_i). Under recurrent strict inadequacy a fixed positive-weight component has positive expected log-growth (g'(0) = p(D) - q(D) - eta > 0), so E diverges almost surely by the strong law. Reject m permanently when log E_i^m >= b_i. In the exact deterministic case, reject at the first zero-probability consequence. The active set is monotone and eventually constant.
+6. Judged updating: the reference posterior updates by token likelihoods marg_i m(C)(a)(x_i); the acting belief is its restriction to the active set. Dissonance is an empty active set. Proposition 6.5: if all profile-adequate theories are active at t_0, Lemma D.1 makes the eventual active set empty almost surely exactly when every theory is eta-inadequate (exposure). For a non-exposed profile, conditional Ville bounds rejection of an active adequate theory by E_i^m(t_0)*exp(-b_i).
+7. Repair (Section 7, Proposition D.2): for each candidate strict refinement P', recode the analyst record and apply the repair test (equation 6) at every nonempty (C',a). Because the constraints involve one cell at a time, M_hat_i^R(P', r_bar_i) is the product over cells of Theta_i^R(C'|r_bar_i), so P' is restorative iff every cell is repair-coherent. Enumerate partitions to obtain the minimally restorative set R(P_i, r_bar_i).
+8. Theorem 7.4: couple two worlds tau != tau' that agree on joint consequence laws at all reachable pairs, using common state draws, common seeds for production, formulation, bridge-prior selection and tie-breaking, and identity-coupled consequence draws. Induct over periods: identical histories give identical partitions, policies and action profiles, hence identical records and outputs. A common output distribution cannot put probability one on two disjoint correctness sets. Proposition 7.5: sufficiency for each candidate is refinement of each P^tau', hence refinement of their join.
+9. Economics of inquiry (Section 8): at a dissonant record, enumerate the deliberative groupings G of the tokens. For each group compute Theta_i(g) by the grouping test (counts >= n_bar) and keep the coherent groupings. For each coherent G compute the per-token robust gain Delta_i^G(t,a_i): the minimum over rival actions and over Theta_i(g(t)) of the payoff difference between a_i and the standing action. Then compute Lfine (best action per current-cell/group intersection), Lcur (best single action per current cell) and J = Lfine - Lcur, and set B_hat_i = (T/|r_i|)*max({0} union {J}).
+10. Proposition 8.4: finiteness makes every term well defined. The sum of maxima is at least the maximum of sums, giving J >= 0. Each per-token gain is at most max pi_i - min pi_i, and the standing action gives Lcur >= 0, so 0 <= B_hat_i <= T*(max pi_i - min pi_i). The election rule is: elect iff lambda_i^(k_i)*B_hat_i >= gamma_i. B_hat_i > 0 iff some coherent G has J > 0.
+11. Pivot (Section 9): after successful production of P_i', the formulation rule draws m' from M_hat_i^R(P_i', r_bar_i) and the agent forms a full-support bridge prior mu_i^+. Then Coh_i = mu_i^+(m') / sum over M_hat_i^R of mu_i^+. For warrant, fix an analyst assessment (calT, nu) and compute W(o) = nu(A_o | r_bar_i, h_i, O_i = o).
+12. Theorem 9.2: with Z = (r_bar_i, h_i), truth-blindness and record-measurability make the output kernel K(.|Z) independent of the candidate. Hence Pr(tau', O in B | Z) = nu(tau'|Z)*K(B|Z), so O is independent of tau' given Z, and W(o) = nu(A_o | Z) after summing over A_o. Corollary 9.3: expressing each candidate requires sufficiency for it, hence refinement of the join of Proposition 7.5.
+13. Dynamics (Section 10, Appendix B): Theorem 10.4(a): the total cell count sum_i |P_i| is an integer in [n, n|S|] that rises at each success and never falls, so there are at most n*(|S| - 1) refinements. (b): since B_hat_i <= T*Delta pi_i, the election condition fails once lambda_i^(k) < gamma_i/(T*Delta pi_i), at a finite k because lambda -> 0; the counter resets only at the finitely many successes. (c): after G* = max(last refinement, last election), languages are fixed and every dissonance event fails the threshold. (d): on the eventually-constant-profile event, couple to an auxiliary constant-profile process and apply Lemma D.1. A nonempty survivor set means never dissonant; an empty one means dissonant stasis.
+14. Supporting results: Proposition D.5(i) holds by continuity of the strict no-switch slack at the limit. D.5(ii-a): in the deterministic case a 0-adequate active theory is never rejected. D.5(ii-b): Ville gives exp(-b_i) per agent and a union bound gives persistence probability >= 1 - sum_i exp(-b_i). D.6 follows from SCUE (iii) and Lemma D.1. D.7: component domination plus bounded-increment Wald reasoning gives E[tau_m] <= 2*(b_i + c_m)/d_i^m, hence O(b_i/D_i), and bounded one-period growth gives Omega(b_i). D.9: diagnosticity implies exposure, which by Lemma D.1 empties the active set, contradicting SCUE (iii). D.10: the strict-punishment inequality makes every expressible theory eta-inadequate, hence exposure and diagnosticity.
+15. Example 1, dominance: at a cell with label beliefs p0, p1, the gain of action 0 over 1 is p0/2 - (1 - beta/2)*p1 against a rival playing 0 and (1 - beta/2)*p0 - p1/2 against a rival playing 1. So 0 is strictly dominant iff p0 > (2 - beta)*p1; at beta = 1, belief asymmetry yields differentiated constant policies.
+16. Example 1, discovery: firm 1 plays 0 and firm 2 plays 1 at states s00 and s11 (T = 2). Firm 1 earns 1 at s00 and 0 at s11, which rejects m^0, m^1 and m^n, so each active set empties. Repairs: of the 15 partitions of 4 states, 10 separate s00 from s11 and the 4 two-cell ones are minimal (row, column, two isolating partitions), so 11 are excluded. The column world agrees on both visited states, so the worst-case success of any truth-blind rule is at most 1/2, and the join of the row and column partitions is the singleton partition.
+17. Example 1, leverage: the winning token pins theta^0 (best gain 0); the losing token pins theta^1 (gain 1/2 against either rival action). So Lfine = 1/2. In the one-cell current language, a uniform reversal nets 1/2 - 1/2 = 0, so Lcur = 0, J = 1/2 and B = (2/2)*(1/2) = 1/2; the firm elects iff lambda^(0)/2 >= gamma. Validation stretch over all four states with T = 2 and |r| = 4 (firm 1 on the row policy, firm 2 on the column policy): firm 1 has Lfine = Lcur = J = B = 0; firm 2 has Lfine = 1, Lcur = 0, J = 1 and B = (2/4)*1 = 1/2.
+18. Example 1, warrant: the column (or row) refinement pins a unique consistent theory, so Coh = 1. With calT = {row world, column world} and a symmetric nu, the diagonal record has equal likelihood in both worlds, so by Theorem 9.2 W = 1/2 and gap = 1 - 1/2 = 1/2.
+19. Example 1, endings: the trap at beta = 1 has both firms on the column policy, pooling everywhere with payoff 1/2, which equals the prediction, so it is a SCUE. The knife edge at beta < 1: pooled off-diagonal play pays beta/2 != 1/2, which triggers dissonance. Persistence at beta < 1: firm 1 on the row law against firm 2 constant at c earns (1 + 1 + 1/2 + 1/2)/4 = 3/4 against (0 + 0 + 1/2 + 1/2)/4 = 1/4. Firm 2's record rejects all constant theories and its balanced record gives B_2 = (2/4)*1 = 1/2. Stasis is permanent if gamma_2 > lambda_2^(k_{2,t_0})*B_hat_2(r_{2,t}) for all later records; gamma_2 > lambda_2^(k_{2,t_0})*T*(max pi_2 - min pi_2) is sufficient.
```

### ai-appendix/scope.md

```diff
--- r1/ai-appendix/scope.md
+++ r2/ai-appendix/scope.md
@@ -2,44 +2,14 @@
 
 Carried verbatim from `appendix/appendix.md#does-not-claim`; the generator does not write this part.
 
-From [Remark 9.6 (What the theorem does and does not claim)](../paper/paper.md#rem-scope) of the paper:
+From [11.3 Limitations and the research program](../paper/paper.md#limitations-and-the-research-program) of the paper:
 
-[Theorem 9.4](../paper/paper.md#thm-absorption) is a statement about the epistemic process—the death
-of inquiry—not about the convergence of play.  Within a Bayesian world,
-committed policies may in principle continue to cycle as beliefs and
-conjectures chase one another; that is the classical convergence problem of
-learning in games (Fudenberg and Levine 1993; Kalai and Lehrer 1993), it is
-orthogonal to this paper's contribution, and the theorem deliberately does
-not resolve it.  Part (d) and the rest-point characterization are
-accordingly conditional on play stabilizing, which in every configuration
-featured in this paper is immediate: their beliefs make one action per cell
-strictly dominant.  Separately, if the success bound in
-[assumption 9.3](../paper/paper.md#ass-regularity)(ii) is dropped, one further absorbing pattern becomes
-possible: perpetually elected, perpetually failing inquiry—a question
-worth asking that is never answered, whose economics (paying $\gamma_i$
-forever for a repair that never arrives) merit separate study.
+The principal ontological boundary combines three restrictions: the elementary mechanism vocabulary is fixed, the analyst's state space is finite and fixed, and semantic production is a primitive operation.
+Novelty is therefore agent-relative: refinement makes an analyst-represented distinction available to the agent but does not create objective states or elementary mechanisms.
+The model can explain why a new representation is demanded and constrained, but not creation of the analyst's ontology or a new kind of causal mechanism.
 
-From [10.3 Limitations and the research program](../paper/paper.md#limitations-and-the-research-program) of the paper:
-
-Four boundaries of the present model define the program's next steps.
-First, the mechanism vocabulary $\Theta$ is fixed: agents lack distinctions,
-never concepts of consequence-generation.  Expanding the vocabulary
-itself—deeper unawareness—is the natural second paper in the sequence,
-and the awareness-transition machinery here is built to receive it.  Second,
-the model prices participation in inquiry but not its intensity or
-duration, and the technology's success is exogenous; endogenizing the depth
-of inquiry connects to models of costly deliberation and planning
-(Ryall 2026), where cached prior conclusions govern when
-reconsideration is rational—the theory-to-plan transition.  Third, agents
-are individuals; the theory-bearing *firm*—how insights become shared
-languages, collective intentions, and organizational commitments—is a
-separate paper resting on the same primitives (Ryall and Wilkins 2018).
-Fourth, the competitive consequences here run through payoffs in a fixed
-game; joining the front end developed here to the cooperative back end of
-value capture (Gans and Ryall 2017; Bryan et al. 2022), where awareness
-itself earns rents and disclosure is strategic, completes the arc.  Within
-the present model, the flagged analytical items are general conditions for
-the convergence of play within Bayesian worlds ([remark 9.6](../paper/paper.md#rem-scope)),
-equilibrium in the anti-coordination region that value destruction creates,
-counterfactual transparency as a design variable, and Markov state
-processes.
+Three extensions follow.
+First, inquiry intensity, duration, and technological success can become endogenous, connecting question valuation to costly deliberation and planning (Ryall 2026).
+Second, replacing individual agents with theory-bearing organizations would study how insights become shared languages, collective intentions, and commitments (Ryall and Wilkins 2018).
+Third, connecting this pre-Bayesian front end to cooperative value capture would allow awareness rents, disclosure, and bargaining over newly represented opportunities (Gans and Ryall 2017; Bryan et al. 2022).
+These extensions can enrich how long inquiry lasts, who bears a theory inside an organization, and how the resulting awareness advantage is appropriated.
```

```diff
@@ -2,17 +2,13 @@
 
 Carried verbatim from `appendix/appendix.md#choices`; the generator does not write this part.
 
-From [3.5 Modeling commitments](../paper/paper.md#sec-commitments) of the paper:
+From [4.6 Scope and modeling commitments](../paper/paper.md#sec-commitments) of the paper:
 
-Five disciplines govern everything that follows; they are what distinguish a
-model of unawareness from a model of inattention or model selection.
-(i) Agents hold no prior over the outputs of the inquiry technology and never
-optimize over named future theories; pre-insight deliberation quantifies only
-over represented objects.  (ii) Questions are generated by defects in the
-current representation, not drawn from a primitive list.  (iii) Inquiry can
-fail, and its successes can be false; nothing reveals the objective
-assignment by construction.  (iv) All valuations attached to inquiry are
-subjective, coarse, and possibly wrong; no objective payoff information
-leaks from behind the awareness frontier.  (v) Evidence that generates a
-candidate representation is separated, in the formal accounting, from
-evidence that warrants it.
+Five disciplines govern everything that follows; they are what distinguish a model of unawareness from a model of inattention or model selection.
+(i) Agents hold no prior over the outputs of the inquiry technology and never optimize over named future theories; pre-insight deliberation quantifies only over represented objects.
+(ii) The questions modeled here are generated by defects in the current representation, not drawn from a primitive list.
+(iii) Inquiry can fail, and its successes can be false; nothing reveals the objective assignment by construction.
+(iv) All valuations attached to inquiry are subjective, coarse, and possibly wrong; no objective payoff information leaks from behind the awareness frontier.
+(v) Evidence that generates a candidate representation is separated, in the formal accounting, from evidence that warrants it.
+
+Expansion of the mechanism vocabulary itself is a deeper dimension of awareness growth deliberately left outside this paper.
```

```diff
-## A. Proofs of Theorem [9.4](../paper/paper.md#thm-absorption) and Proposition [9.5](../paper/paper.md#prop-rest-points) {#app-absorption}
+## A. The evidence process {#app-eprocess}
+
+Fix agent $i$ and theory $m$.
+At a visited pair $(C,a)$ write $p=p_i^{*}(\cdot\mid C,a)$ and $q=\marg{i}m(C)(a)$; adequacy at $(C,a)$ is $\TV(p,q)\le\eta$, equivalently $\lvert p(D)-q(D)\rvert\le\eta$ for every $D\subseteq X_i$.
+For each such $D$ and each betting fraction $\lambda$, retain the one-sided factor
+
+$$
+F_{\lambda,D}(x)=1+\lambda\bigl(\mathbf 1\{x\in D\}-(q(D)+\eta)\bigr).
+$$
+
+Fix $0<\lambda_{\max}\le1/(1+\eta)$, choose a countable dense set $\Lambda\subset(0,\lambda_{\max})$, and assign every $\lambda\in\Lambda$ a strictly positive atomic weight, with the weights summing to one.
+Assign every $D\subseteq X_i$ strictly positive weight as well, and let $M_i^{m,(C,a)}$ be the resulting convex mixture of the fixed processes that multiply by $F_{\lambda,D}(x)$ at each visit to $(C,a)$ and by $1$ otherwise.
+Because $D$ ranges over all subsets of $X_i$, a negative discrepancy on $D$ is a positive discrepancy on $D^c$, so no separate sign mixture is needed.
+Under adequacy, $p(D)\le q(D)+\eta$ and hence $\mathbb E_p[F_{\lambda,D}]\le1$, so every fixed component is a nonnegative supermartingale and their convex mixture remains one.
+Assign every visited-pair component a strictly positive weight and set
+
+$$
+E_i^{m}=\sum_{(C,a)}v_{(C,a)}\,M_i^{m,(C,a)},
+\qquad
+\textstyle\sum_{(C,a)}v_{(C,a)}=1,\ v_{(C,a)}>0.
+$$
+
+As a convex combination of nonnegative supermartingales, $E_i^{m}$ is a nonnegative supermartingale under the null that $m$ is adequate at every pair, with $E_i^{m}=1$ before play; Ville's inequality then gives $\Pr(\sup_t E_i^{m}\ge e^{b_i})\le e^{-b_i}$, the false-alarm bound of [definition 6.1](../paper/paper.md#def-test).
+If instead $m$ is strictly inadequate at a recurrent pair $j^{*}=(C,a)$, some $D$ satisfies $p(D)>q(D)+\eta$.
+For that event define
+
+$$
+g(\lambda)=\mathbb E_p\log\!\left(1+\lambda[\mathbf 1_D-(q(D)+\eta)]\right).
+$$
+
+Then $g(0)=0$ and $g'(0)=p(D)-q(D)-\eta>0$, so every sufficiently small positive $\lambda$ has $g(\lambda)>0$.
+Density of $\Lambda$ supplies a positive-weight atom $\lambda^{*}$ with strictly positive expected log-growth; if $W^{*}$ is its fixed component and $\pi^{*}>0$ its weight within the pair mixture, the strong law under recurrent visitation gives $W^{*}(t)\to\infty$ almost surely.
+Because the global process assigns the pair fixed weight $v_{j^{*}}>0$, pathwise
+
+$$
+E_i^{m}(t)\ge v_{j^{*}}\pi^{*}W^{*}(t)\longrightarrow\infty\quad\text{a.s.}
+$$
+
+The positive per-period log-growth rate of such a component is its *betting exponent* (and the supremum across available components is the optimal log-growth exponent); this is the dominating-component property used in [lemma D.1](#lem-test-consistency) and [proposition D.7](#prop-capacity).
+
+The exact deterministic case $\eta=0$ uses a separate zero-likelihood detector rather than the stochastic mixture above.
+Conceptually, $E_i^m(t)=1$ until the first observed consequence to which theory $m$ assigns probability zero, at which point $E_i^m(t)=+\infty$ and remains there.
+A $0$-adequate deterministic theory never encounters such an event and is never falsely rejected, whereas a $0$-inadequate theory at a recurrent pair encounters a diagnostic impossible consequence almost surely and is rejected at its first occurrence.
+Thus $b_i$ has no effect in the deterministic case.
+
+## B. Proofs of Theorem [10.4](../paper/paper.md#thm-absorption) and Proposition [D.5](#prop-rest-points) {#app-absorption}
 
 ### Probability framework {#probability-framework}
 
-All randomness is carried by three mutually independent i.i.d. families on
-a common probability space: state draws $\{s_{g,t}\}$ over generations $g$
-and episodes $t\leq T$, with law $\rho$; consequence randomizers
-$\{U_{g,t}\}$, uniform on $[0,1]$, from which $x_{g,t}$ is generated through
-a fixed measurable representation of the kernel
-$\tau(s_{g,t})(a_{g,t})$; and production randomizers $\{V_{i,g}\}$, uniform
-on $[0,1]$ ([assumption 9.3](../paper/paper.md#ass-regularity)(ii)).  Let $\mathcal F_g$ denote the
-$\sigma$-algebra generated by all randomness through generation $g$.
-Committed policies and election indicators for generation $g$ are
-$\mathcal F_{g-1}$-measurable, since they are functions of records, beliefs,
-and conjectures formed from earlier generations; conditional on
-$\mathcal F_{g-1}$ and on an election by agent $i$, production succeeds with
-probability at least $\underline\pi$.
+All randomness is carried by three mutually independent i.i.d. families on a common probability space: state draws $\{s_t\}$ over periods $t=1,2,\dots$, with law $\rho$; consequence randomizers $\{U_t\}$, uniform on $[0,1]$, from which $x_t$ is generated through a fixed measurable representation of the kernel $\tau(s_t)(a_t)$; and production randomizers $\{V_{i,t}\}$, uniform on $[0,1]$, drawn when agent $i$ elects inquiry ([assumption 10.3](../paper/paper.md#ass-regularity)(ii)).
+Let $\mathcal F_t$ denote the $\sigma$-algebra generated by all randomness through period $t$.
+Each agent's standing policy and election indicator in period $t$ are $\mathcal F_{t-1}$-measurable, since they are functions of records, beliefs, and conjectures formed from earlier periods; dissonance and the election in period $t$ are thus decided on the record through period $t-1$.
+Conditional on $\mathcal F_{t-1}$ and on an election by agent $i$ at a repairable record, production succeeds with probability at least $\underline\pi$; at an irreparable record it fails surely.

+The total cell count $\sum_i\lvert\mathcal P_i\rvert$ is integer valued, starts at no less than $n$, never exceeds $n\lvert S\rvert$, and strictly increases at every success.

+After $G^{*}$, no language changes and no inquiry cost is paid.
+Because the election rule is mechanical—agent $i$ elects exactly when it is dissonant and $\lambda_i^{(k_i)}\widehat B_i\geq\gamma_i$—every dissonance event after $G^{*}$ must fail the participation threshold.
```
