```diff
-  - id: D15
-    kind: proof
-    states: paper/paper.md#prop-rest-points
-    symbols: {}
-    hypotheses:
-      - "(i) awareness partitions, committed policies, conjectures and acting beliefs are all eventually constant almost surely"
-      - "(ii) the epistemic states satisfy the SCUE conditions, play generates only records with B_i = 0, and each agent's committed action at each cell is strictly optimal under every conjecture and every theory in its active set"
-      - "(ii-a) deterministic mechanisms"
-      - "(ii-b) stochastic mechanisms; every theory in every active set is eta'-adequate under the configuration's play for some eta' < eta; the strict optimality extends to every (eta + epsilon)-adequate theory for some epsilon in (0, 2(eta - eta')]; the configuration is reached with at least N(delta) observations at every recurrent pair"
-    steps:
-      - says: (i) Condition (i) of Definition 9.1 holds because each generation's committed policy solves equation (3) at the then-current belief and conjecture, which are eventually the limits.
-        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#proof-of-proposition-i, paper/paper.md#def-scue, paper/paper.md#ass-commitment]
-      - says: (i) Empirical conjectures converge almost surely to the frequencies induced by the eventually-constant profile, and an eventually-constant conjecture equals its limit, so q_i agrees with induced play at every on-path cell, which is condition (ii).
-        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#proof-of-proposition-i, paper/paper.md#def-scue, paper/paper.md#sec-bayesian]
-      - says: (i) An agent not in dissonant stasis is eventually never dissonant by Theorem 9.4(d); judged updating keeps the support of the acting belief inside the active set, which is eventually A_i*, whose members are eta-adequate at every recurrent pair, which is condition (iii).
-        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#proof-of-proposition-i, paper/paper.md#thm-absorption, paper/paper.md#judged-updating]
-      - says: (i) Elections cease by Theorem 9.4(b), so whenever dissonance occurs the participation threshold fails; absent dissonance, B_i = 0 by Definition 7.3, which is condition (iv).
-        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#proof-of-proposition-i, paper/paper.md#thm-absorption, paper/paper.md#def-leverage]
-      - says: (ii-a) With deterministic mechanisms, SCUE condition (iii) forces every on-path observation to match the active theories exactly, since an active theory's prediction at a visited pair is degenerate and a single disagreeing observation would reject it permanently; so empirical conditionals are constant and active sets never change; dissonance never occurs; B_i = 0 at every generated record gives no election and no refinement; and strict optimality of the committed action under every conjecture and every active theory fixes sigma_i however the reference posterior and the empirical conjectures evolve, so the SCUE conditions continue to hold with conjectures converging to the constant induced play.
-        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#proof-of-proposition-ii-a, paper/paper.md#def-scue, paper/paper.md#def-test, paper/paper.md#ass-elective]
-      - says: (ii-b) Fix epsilon in (0, 2(eta - eta')] and choose N so that w(n) <= epsilon/2 for all n >= N and the geometric tail sum_{n >= N} K e^{-n epsilon^2/2} is at most delta/P, K the constant of the finite-alphabet Hoeffding bound and P the number of recurrent pairs across agents; the event that some recurrent pair's empirical conditional ever leaves the epsilon/2-ball around its on-path conditional after count N then has probability at most delta.
-        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#proof-of-proposition-ii-b, derivations:D22, "cited: Hoeffding's inequality, as a union of bounds over the events of the finite space X_i"]
-      - says: (ii-b) On the complement, every eta'-adequate theory passes the test at every recurrent pair since TV <= epsilon/2 + eta' <= eta, and frozen constraints are unchanged and already satisfied, so active sets remain nonempty and dissonance never occurs; every theory the test admits is (eta + epsilon)-adequate since TV <= eta + w(n) + epsilon/2 <= eta + epsilon, so the extended strict-optimality hypothesis fixes each committed action for every conjecture and every acting belief the judged update can produce; play never changes, the generated records have B_i = 0 so no election or refinement occurs, and the SCUE conditions hold in every subsequent generation with conjectures converging to the constant induced play.
-        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#proof-of-proposition-ii-b, paper/paper.md#def-test, paper/paper.md#def-scue, paper/paper.md#ass-elective, derivations:D22]
-    conclusion: A converging configuration limits, off the agents in dissonant stasis, to a SCUE; a zero-leverage SCUE with strict optimality margins persists, surely under deterministic mechanisms and, under stochastic mechanisms with the stated margins, with probability at least 1 - delta once every recurrent pair carries N(delta) observations.
-
-  - id: D16
-    kind: proof
-    states: paper/paper.md#lem-test-consistency
-    symbols: {}
-    hypotheses:
-      - a committed profile played in all generations
-      - "no theory's distance to an on-path conditional equals eta exactly"
-    steps:
-      - says: Visited pairs accumulate observations without bound almost surely under i.i.d. full-support states, so the empirical conditional at each visited pair converges to the on-path conditional by the strong law of large numbers.
-        uses: [paper/paper.md#sec-primitives, paper/paper.md#on-path-conditionals, paper/paper.md#def-test, "cited: the strong law of large numbers"]
-      - says: The triangle inequality places the total-variation distance from the empirical conditional to a theory's prediction within any epsilon > 0 of the distance from the on-path conditional from some generation on.
-        uses: [paper/paper.md#def-test]
-      - says: The genericity hypothesis converts strict inequalities into eventual acceptance and rejection, and the slack sequence absorbs finite-sample fluctuation before that generation.
-        uses: [paper/paper.md#def-test]
-    conclusion: Almost surely every eta-adequate theory is eventually always in the active set and every eta-inadequate theory is eventually never in it.
-
-  - id: D17
-    kind: proof
-    states: paper/paper.md#prop-coherence
-    symbols: {}
-    hypotheses: ["a refinement P' of P_i, the retained record recoded to its cells"]
-    steps:
-      - says: "Theories factor across cells: M(P') = Theta^{P'}, and the constraints of equation (5) apply cell by cell."
-        uses: [paper/paper.md#def-awareness, paper/paper.md#def-test]
-      - says: So a passing assignment exists exactly when a passing mechanism can be chosen for each cell independently, that is, exactly when every cell is coherent.
-        uses: [paper/paper.md#def-test]
-    conclusion: The recoded active set on P' is nonempty if and only if every cell of P' is coherent.
-
-  - id: D18
-    kind: proof
-    states: paper/paper.md#prop-adequacy
-    symbols: {}
-    hypotheses: ["in a SCUE"]
-    steps:
-      - says: Definition 9.1(iii) supports each acting belief on eta-adequate theories, and the test-consistency lemma makes the surviving active set consist of theories eta-adequate at every visited pair.
-        uses: [paper/paper.md#def-scue, paper/paper.md#lem-test-consistency]
-      - says: "The residual clause is the complement of the visited pairs' constraints: falsity survives only at states pooled with causally different states whose mixture imitates a single mechanism at the visited profiles, and in counterfactual claims at unvisited profiles."
-        uses: [paper/paper.md#def-exposure, paper/paper.md#on-path-conditionals]
-    conclusion: In a SCUE every theory in the support of every acting belief is eta-adequate at every visited pair, and surviving falsity is confined to what the configuration does not expose.
+      - eventually constant almost surely
+      - strict no-switch point at the limit
+      - satisfy the SCUE conditions
+      - strictly optimal under every conjecture and every theory in its active set
+    steps:
+      - says: (i) Continuity of the finite-dimensional payoff comparison carries the strict no-switch slack to the limit, so the eventually constant policy is stationary under the switching rule; empirical conjectures converge to the induced frequencies; non-stasis agents are eventually never dissonant, with acting beliefs on the eta-adequate survivor set; elections cease. The limit is a partial SCUE.
+        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#proof-of-proposition-i, paper/paper.md#def-scue, paper/paper.md#thm-absorption, paper/paper.md#ass-commitment]
+      - says: (ii-a) With deterministic mechanisms rejection is irreversible; SCUE condition (iii) gives an active 0-adequate theory, which is never rejected, so the active set stays nonempty; dissonance never occurs, B_i = 0 gives no election, and strict optimality fixes the policy as the set shrinks and conjectures converge.
+        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#proof-of-proposition-ii-a, paper/paper.md#def-scue, paper/paper.md#conv-calibration]
+      - says: (ii-b) Condition on the entry history and couple to the fixed-profile auxiliary process; each entry-active eta-adequate theory with E <= 1 is rejected with probability at most e^{-b_i} by Ville's inequality; a union bound over agents leaves probability at least 1 - sum_i e^{-b_i} that no agent is ever dissonant; on that event strict optimality fixes play, B_i = 0 stops elections, and the SCUE conditions hold in every period; in the deterministic case surely.
+        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#proof-of-proposition-ii-b, appendix/appendix.md#app-eprocess, derivations:D4, paper/paper.md#def-scue]
+    conclusion: Converging non-stasis configurations limit to a partial SCUE, and a zero-leverage SCUE with strict optimality margins persists, exactly under deterministic mechanisms and with probability at least 1 - sum_i e^{-b_i} otherwise.
 
   - id: D19
     kind: proof
-    states: paper/paper.md#prop-persistence
-    symbols: {}
-    hypotheses: ["the laboratory of Example 1 at beta = 1; the proof is by instance"]
-    steps:
-      - says: "Instance: firm 1 holds the row refinement and the true assignment and plays the row law; firm 2 remains coarse and plays a constant architecture; the firms pool at two states and differentiate at two, firm 1 winning both, so expected per-generation payoffs are 3/4 and 1/4 under uniform rho, with different terminal partitions and theories."
-        uses: [paper/paper.md#example-1-continued-6, appendix/appendix.md#persistence-payoffs, derivations:D6]
-      - says: Firm 2's record rejects all three constant theories (it wins nothing differentiated and loses twice), so it sits in dissonance with B_2 > 0, and it has no priced repair whenever gamma_2 > lambda_2 B_2.
-        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#persistence-payoffs, appendix/appendix.md#unit-leverage, derivations:D2, paper/paper.md#prop-participation]
-      - says: The difference is permanent, since firm 1 is never dissonant and firm 2 abstains at every dissonant generation and carries its policy forward, so firm 2 sits in dissonant stasis and the configuration is absorbing.
-        uses: [paper/paper.md#example-1-continued-6, paper/paper.md#ass-elective, paper/paper.md#ass-commitment, paper/paper.md#thm-absorption]
-    conclusion: There exist primitives and absorbing configurations with different terminal partitions and theories and strictly, permanently different expected payoffs in which no disadvantaged agent has a priced repair.
+    states: appendix/appendix.md#prop-adequacy
+    symbols: {}
+    hypotheses: ["In a SCUE"]
+    steps:
+      - says: The first claim is condition (iii) of the SCUE definition.
+        uses: [paper/paper.md#def-scue]
+      - says: Under strict adequacy, or in the exact deterministic case, the test-consistency lemma makes the active set eventually constant and excludes every eta-inadequate theory; judged updating restricts the acting belief to that set.
+        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#lem-test-consistency, paper/paper.md#judged-updating]
+      - says: The residual clause is the complement of the on-path constraints.
+        uses: [paper/paper.md#def-exposure]
+    conclusion: In a SCUE acting-belief mass on inadequate theories vanishes, and surviving falsity is confined to what the configuration does not expose.
 
   - id: D20
     kind: proof
-    states: paper/paper.md#prop-diagnostic
-    symbols: {}
-    hypotheses: ["a diagnostic environment (Definition 9.10)"]
-    steps:
-      - says: By Definition 9.1(iii), theories surviving in a SCUE are not eta-inadequate; in a diagnostic environment every payoff-relevantly false theory is; so no SCUE carries payoff-relevant error.
-        uses: [paper/paper.md#def-scue, paper/paper.md#def-diagnostic]
-      - says: "The second claim restates Definition 5.4: a false theory survives only under concealing profiles, and Proposition 5.5 makes concealment necessary as well as sufficient."
-        uses: [paper/paper.md#def-exposure, paper/paper.md#prop-exposure]
-    conclusion: In a diagnostic environment every SCUE is free of payoff-relevant error; payoff-relevant falsity survives only in environments that admit concealing profiles and requires them to be sustained.

-# ----- further closed forms ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+    states: appendix/appendix.md#prop-capacity
+    symbols: {}
+    hypotheses: ["stochastic mechanisms satisfying"]
+    steps:
+      - says: (i) Exposure makes d_i^m > 0 for every expressible theory and finiteness makes D_i > 0; for each theory choose a recurrent inadequate pair and a positive-weight betting component whose per-period expected log-growth is at least d_i^m / 2.
+        uses: [paper/paper.md#def-exposure, appendix/appendix.md#app-eprocess]
+      - says: Component domination bounds the rejection time by the crossing time of that component's log above b_i minus the log of its weights; bounded log-increments and Wald reasoning give E[tau_m] <= 2(b_i + c_m) / d_i^m; the dissonance time is at most the sum over finitely many theories, so E[tau] = O(b_i / D_i).
+        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#app-eprocess, paper/paper.md#def-test, "cited: Wald's identity for stopping times of processes with bounded increments"]
+      - says: Bounded one-period growth of each freshly initialized evidence process means none can cross e^{b_i} before a number of periods proportional to b_i, so E[tau] = Omega(b_i).
+        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#app-eprocess]
+      - says: (ii) is the per-agent bound of the rest-points proposition; neither rate argument applies to the separate exact deterministic detector.
+        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#prop-rest-points, paper/paper.md#conv-calibration]
+    conclusion: Detection time is Theta(b_i) up to the growth rate D_i, and false dissonance at a non-exposed profile has probability at most e^{-b_i}.

   - id: D22
-    # The count threshold of Proposition 9.5(ii-b): the two margins the proof needs (an eta'-adequate theory passes,
-    # TV <= eps/2 + eta' <= eta; an admitted theory is (eta + eps)-adequate, TV <= eta + w(n) + eps/2 <= eta + eps) and the
-    # Hoeffding tail whose geometric sum the choice of N(delta) drives below delta/P.
-    states: appendix/appendix.md#proof-of-proposition-ii-b
-    symbols:
-      eta: {domain: positive, meaning: the test tolerance eta}
-      etap: {domain: nonnegative, meaning: "the tighter adequacy level eta' < eta of the active theories"}
-      eps: {domain: positive, meaning: "the margin epsilon in (0, 2(eta - eta')]"}
-      w: {domain: nonnegative, meaning: the slack w(n) at a count n >= N, at most eps/2 by the choice of N}
-      K: {domain: positive, meaning: the constant of the finite-alphabet Hoeffding bound}
-      N: {domain: positive, meaning: the count threshold N(delta)}
-      P: {domain: positive, meaning: the number of recurrent pairs across agents}
-    assumptions: ["eta > 0", "etap >= 0", "etap < eta", "eps > 0", "eps <= 2*(eta - etap)", "w >= 0", "w <= eps/2", "K > 0", "N > 0", "P > 0"]
-    quantities:
-      passing_distance_bound: "eps/2 + etap"
-      passing_slack: "eta - (eps/2 + etap)"
-      passing_distance_at_largest_eps: "2*(eta - etap)/2 + etap"
-      admitted_adequacy_bound: "eta + w + eps/2"
-      admitted_slack: "(eta + eps) - (eta + w + eps/2)"
-      per_count_bound: "K*exp(-N*eps**2/2)"
-      log_per_count_bound: "log(K) - N*eps**2/2"
-      tail_sum: "K*exp(-N*eps**2/2)/(1 - exp(-eps**2/2))"
-      union_over_pairs: "P*K*exp(-N*eps**2/2)/(1 - exp(-eps**2/2))"
-    stated:
-      passing_distance_at_largest_eps: "eta"
-      admitted_slack: "eps/2 - w"
-    comparative_statics:
-      - {of: passing_slack, wrt: eps, stated_sign: negative}
-      - {of: log_per_count_bound, wrt: N, stated_sign: negative}
-    examples:
-      # the margins are tight at eps = 2(eta - eta') and w = eps/2
-      - params: {eta: 0.1, etap: 0.05, eps: 0.1, w: 0.05}
-        expected: {passing_distance_bound: 0.1, passing_slack: 0, admitted_adequacy_bound: 0.2, admitted_slack: 0}
+    kind: proof
+    states: appendix/appendix.md#prop-punishing
+    symbols: {}
+    hypotheses: ["strictly punishing"]
+    steps:
+      - says: At a payoff-relevantly erroneous state, the displayed inequality places the on-path conditional more than eta from the prediction of every expressible theory at the induced recurrent pair, so every m is eta-inadequate and P_i is exposed.
+        uses: [paper/paper.md#def-exposure, paper/paper.md#def-test]
+      - says: This implication holds for every profile satisfying the strategic stationarity and conjecture-confirmation conditions, so the primitives are diagnostic.
+        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#def-diagnostic]
+      - says: The final claim follows from the no-error proposition for diagnostic environments.
+        uses: [appendix/appendix.md#prop-diagnostic]
+    conclusion: Strictly punishing primitives are diagnostic, so every SCUE under them is free of payoff-relevant policy error.
```
